# Höpfner, Der Krieg von 1806 und 1807, Bd. 1 (1850): selections for the fourth-coalition 1806 first pass (Saalfeld, Jena and Auerstedt), v1

## transcription-note

Digital OCR (Tesseract 5.0.0-rc2), as served by the Internet Archive, of a library copy digitised by Google; not verified against print. The text is set in Fraktur, and the OCR misreads many letters (among them the long s as 'f', 'k' as 'f' or 'T', 'd' as 'b' or 'v', 'ck' as 'd' and 'W' as 'B'), splits and joins words and garbles some figures. Whitespace collapsed; all other characters retained, including OCR errors, split words, hyphenation marks, page numbers, footnotes, the printer's signature marks interleaved with the text. Local headings are editorial. Nine bounded passages: the list of misprints to be corrected before reading ('Druckfehler'), in the front matter; Prince Hohenlohe's order to Prince Louis and his letter to the Duke of Brunswick of 9 October (pp. 240-241); the prince's decision, Napoleon's orders to Marshal Lannes and the action at Saalfeld, with its losses and Höpfner's assessment (pp. 260-286); the night of 13-14 October at Hohenlohe's headquarters and at Napoleon's, with Napoleon's orders (pp. 354-357); the main army's disposition for 13 October, its march to Auerstedt, the intelligence of Marshal Davout's corps, the disposition for 14 October and the night at Auerstedt and at Naumburg (pp. 359-366); the battle of Jena, from the opening of chapter 8 to the retreat after General Rüchel's defeat (pp. 369-411); the battle of Auerstedt, from the opening of chapter 9 to the end of the pursuit, with the fight on the Prussian right at Sulza (pp. 429-471); the opening of chapter 10, Höpfner's reflections, to his judgment on the Duke's prohibition of a serious engagement (pp. 473-475); and his reflections on Auerstedt, from the arrangements of 13 October to the abandonment of the retreat over the Unstrut (pp. 481-487). Page locators are read from the page boundaries of the Internet Archive's djvu.xml (leaf n = printed page + 20 to p. 315 and + 21 from p. 316, the scan repeating p. 317 on leaf 336; checked against the OCR page numbers and the registered page images) and are not checked against print unless a figures transcription from the page image is cited.

Noch in der Nacht fandte der Fürſt, der feine Anficht geän- dert hatte, die Depeichen bed Herrn v. Montesquieu durch einen Kourier an den König. — Um Mitternacht Fehrte der Rittmeifler Derfchau vom General Gr. Tauengien zurüd und brachte die Nachricht, daß der Feind fih auf dem Landgrafenberge verftärfe, daß er ſelbſt deſſen Be- wegungen geſehen und zugleich gehört habe, wie man an ven Wegen arbeite, um Kanonen auf die Höhe zu bringen. Der Reit der Nacht wurde zu Kapellenvorf in völliger Uns willenheit über das, was am folgenden Tage bevorfland, vers Ihlummert; man vermutbete, daß fi die franzöffchen Haupt⸗ 355 maffen nach Leipzig und Naumburg wenden würden, und glaubte baber am 14. nur durch Scheingefechte befchäftigt zu werben.
